MC	<p>d0cc_23c4</p> <h4>Three-Point Test Cross Problem</h4><p>A test cross is a way to explore the relationship between genes and their respective alleles. It is a useful tool for genetic mapping and deciphering the inheritance of traits. Specifically, a three-point test cross examines three (3) genes at the same time to learn about their assortment in gamete formation.</p><p>A standard three-point test cross involves crossing a heterozygous organism for all three genes with an organism that is homozygous recessive for all three genes</p> <p>For this problem, a test cross using a fruit fly (<i>Drosophila melanogaster</i>) heterozygous for three genes was conducted to understand their genetic interactions.</p><h6>Characteristics of Recessive Phenotypes</h6><p><ul><li><strong><span style="color: #785814;">Gene E</span></strong> is analogous to the '<span style="color: #785814;"><i>eery</i></span>' phenotype. A fruit fly that is homozygous recessive for <span style="color: #785814;">Gene E</span> appears to have something off, crooked limbs and other twisted appendages.</li> <li><strong><span style="color: #1e6d55;">Gene P</span></strong> is associated with the '<span style="color: #1e6d55;"><i>prickly</i></span>' phenotype. A fruit fly that is homozygous recessive for <span style="color: #1e6d55;">Gene P</span> is covered with sharp bristles, giving it a spiky texture.</li> <li><strong><span style="color: #93376b;">Gene W</span></strong> is correlated with the '<span style="color: #93376b;"><i>waxy</i></span>' phenotype. A fruit fly that is homozygous recessive for <span style="color: #93376b;">Gene W</span> has a thick protective layer that is water resistant and opague.</li> </ul></p><table style="border-collapse: collapse; It is a useful tool for genetic mapping and deciphering the inheritance of traits. Specifically, a three-point test cross examines three (3) genes at the same time to learn about their assortment in gamete formation.</p><p>A standard three-point test cross involves crossing a heterozygous organism for all three genes with an organism that is homozygous recessive for all three genes</p> <p>For this problem, a test cross using a fruit fly (<i>Drosophila melanogaster</i>) heterozygous for three genes was conducted to understand their genetic interactions.</p><h6>Characteristics of Recessive Phenotypes</h6><p><ul><li><strong><span style="color: #695f00;">Gene H</span></strong> is linked with the '<span style="color: #695f00;"><i>horsey</i></span>' phenotype. A fruit fly that is homozygous recessive for <span style="color: #695f00;">Gene H</span> is quite big and strong-looking, much larger than your typical fruit fly.</li> <li><strong><span style="color: #00697b;">Gene M</span></strong> is linked with the '<span style="color: #00697b;"><i>mushy</i></span>' phenotype. A fruit fly that is homozygous recessive for <span style="color: #00697b;">Gene M</span> feels soft to the touch and unusually squishy, unlike the usual firmness.</li> <li><strong><span style="color: #6d44a8;">Gene T</span></strong> is analogous to the '<span style="color: #6d44a8;"><i>tipsy</i></span>' phenotype. MC	<p>2bf6_10af</p> <h4>Three-Point Test Cross Problem</h4><p>A test cross is a way to explore the relationship between genes and their respective alleles. It is a useful tool for genetic mapping and deciphering the inheritance of traits. Specifically, a three-point test cross examines three (3) genes at the same time to learn about their assortment in gamete formation.</p><p>A standard three-point test cross involves crossing a heterozygous organism for all three genes with an organism that is homozygous recessive for all three genes</p> <p>For this problem, a test cross using a fruit fly (<i>Drosophila melanogaster</i>) heterozygous for three genes was conducted to understand their genetic interactions.</p><h6>Characteristics of Recessive Phenotypes</h6><p><ul><li><strong><span style="color: #9b3b19;">Gene D</span></strong> is connected with the '<span style="color: #9b3b19;"><i>dewy</i></span>' phenotype. A fruit fly that is homozygous recessive for <span style="color: #9b3b19;">Gene D</span> appears moist, with its body covered in tiny droplets of water.</li> <li><strong><span style="color: #51671f;">Gene K</span></strong> is associated with the '<span style="color: #51671f;"><i>kidney</i></span>' phenotype. A fruit fly that is homozygous recessive for <span style="color: #51671f;">Gene K</span> has a body shape that is curved, similar to a kidney bean.</li> <li><strong><span style="color: #464cc7;">Gene P</span></strong> is associated with the '<span style="color: #464cc7;"><i>prickly</i></span>' phenotype. A fruit fly that is homozygous recessive for <span style="color: #464cc7;">Gene P</span> is covered with sharp bristles, giving it a spiky texture.</li> </ul></p><table style="border-collapse: collapse; It is a useful tool for genetic mapping and deciphering the inheritance of traits. Specifically, a three-point test cross examines three (3) genes at the same time to learn about their assortment in gamete formation.</p><p>A standard three-point test cross involves crossing a heterozygous organism for all three genes with an organism that is homozygous recessive for all three genes</p> <p>For this problem, a test cross using a fruit fly (<i>Drosophila melanogaster</i>) heterozygous for three genes was conducted to understand their genetic interactions.</p><h6>Characteristics of Recessive Phenotypes</h6><p><ul><li><strong><span style="color: #9e3243;">Gene E</span></strong> is linked with the '<span style="color: #9e3243;"><i>eery</i></span>' phenotype. A fruit fly that is homozygous recessive for <span style="color: #9e3243;">Gene E</span> appears to have something off, crooked limbs and other twisted appendages.</li> <li><strong><span style="color: #695f00;">Gene K</span></strong> is associated with the '<span style="color: #695f00;"><i>kidney</i></span>' phenotype. A fruit fly that is homozygous recessive for <span style="color: #695f00;">Gene K</span> has a body shape that is curved, similar to a kidney bean.</li> <li><strong><span style="color: #00697b;">Gene M</span></strong> is correlated with the '<span style="color: #00697b;"><i>mushy</i></span>' phenotype. A fruit fly that is homozygous recessive for <span style="color: #00697b;">Gene M</span> feels soft to the touch and unusually squishy, unlike the usual firmness.</li> </ul></p><table style="border-collapse: collapse; MC	<p>fe38_23c4</p> <h4>Three-Point Test Cross Problem</h4><p>A test cross is a way to explore the relationship between genes and their respective alleles. It is a useful tool for genetic mapping and deciphering the inheritance of traits. Specifically, a three-point test cross examines three (3) genes at the same time to learn about their assortment in gamete formation.</p><p>A standard three-point test cross involves crossing a heterozygous organism for all three genes with an organism that is homozygous recessive for all three genes</p> <p>For this problem, a test cross using a fruit fly (<i>Drosophila melanogaster</i>) heterozygous for three genes was conducted to understand their genetic interactions.</p><h6>Characteristics of Recessive Phenotypes</h6><p><ul><li><strong><span style="color: #785814;">Gene D</span></strong> is related to the '<span style="color: #785814;"><i>dewy</i></span>' phenotype. A fruit fly that is homozygous recessive for <span style="color: #785814;">Gene D</span> appears moist, with its body covered in tiny droplets of water.</li> <li><strong><span style="color: #006c69;">Gene J</span></strong> is associated with the '<span style="color: #006c69;"><i>jerky</i></span>' phenotype. A fruit fly that is homozygous recessive for <span style="color: #006c69;">Gene J</span> moves in rapid and sudden movements, displaying an unpredictable flight pattern.</li> <li><strong><span style="color: #93376b;">Gene X</span></strong> is affiliated with the '<span style="color: #93376b;"><i>xanthic</i></span>' phenotype. A fruit fly that is homozygous recessive for <span style="color: #93376b;">Gene X</span> has a fluorescent bright yellow coloring.</li> </ul></p><table style="border-collapse: collapse; border: 2px solid black;"><tr><th align="center" style="border: 1px solid black; Specifically, a three-point test cross examines three (3) genes at the same time to learn about their assortment in gamete formation.</p><p>A standard three-point test cross involves crossing a heterozygous organism for all three genes with an organism that is homozygous recessive for all three genes</p> <p>For this problem, a test cross using a fruit fly (<i>Drosophila melanogaster</i>) heterozygous for three genes was conducted to understand their genetic interactions.</p><h6>Characteristics of Recessive Phenotypes</h6><p><ul><li><strong><span style="color: #785814;">Gene H</span></strong> is linked with the '<span style="color: #785814;"><i>horsey</i></span>' phenotype. A fruit fly that is homozygous recessive for <span style="color: #785814;">Gene H</span> is quite big and strong-looking, much larger than your typical fruit fly.</li> <li><strong><span style="color: #1e6d55;">Gene K</span></strong> is linked with the '<span style="color: #1e6d55;"><i>kidney</i></span>' phenotype. A fruit fly that is homozygous recessive for <span style="color: #1e6d55;">Gene K</span> has a body shape that is curved, similar to a kidney bean.</li> <li><strong><span style="color: #464cc7;">Gene X</span></strong> is analogous to the '<span style="color: #464cc7;"><i>xanthic</i></span>' phenotype. A fruit fly that is homozygous recessive for <span style="color: #464cc7;">Gene X</span> has a fluorescent bright yellow coloring.</li> </ul></p><table style="border-collapse: collapse; border: 2px solid black;"><tr><th align="center" style="border: 1px solid black; MC	<p>a6b5_23c4</p> <h4>Three-Point Test Cross Problem</h4><p>A test cross is a way to explore the relationship between genes and their respective alleles. It is a useful tool for genetic mapping and deciphering the inheritance of traits. Specifically, a three-point test cross examines three (3) genes at the same time to learn about their assortment in gamete formation.</p><p>A standard three-point test cross involves crossing a heterozygous organism for all three genes with an organism that is homozygous recessive for all three genes</p> <p>For this problem, a test cross using a fruit fly (<i>Drosophila melanogaster</i>) heterozygous for three genes was conducted to understand their genetic interactions.</p><h6>Characteristics of Recessive Phenotypes</h6><p><ul><li><strong><span style="color: #9b3b19;">Gene B</span></strong> is correlated with the '<span style="color: #9b3b19;"><i>bumpy</i></span>' phenotype. A fruit fly that is homozygous recessive for <span style="color: #9b3b19;">Gene B</span> has a skin texture that is not smooth, but rough with small bumps all over.</li> <li><strong><span style="color: #2a6e36;">Gene E</span></strong> is analogous to the '<span style="color: #2a6e36;"><i>eery</i></span>' phenotype. A fruit fly that is homozygous recessive for <span style="color: #2a6e36;">Gene E</span> appears to have something off, crooked limbs and other twisted appendages.</li> <li><strong><span style="color: #464cc7;">Gene F</span></strong> is associated with the '<span style="color: #464cc7;"><i>fuzzy</i></span>' phenotype. A fruit fly that is homozygous recessive for <span style="color: #464cc7;">Gene F</span> is covered in a dense layer of hairs, giving it a soft appearance.</li> </ul></p><table style="border-collapse: collapse; MC	<p>bc16_10af</p> <h4>Three-Point Test Cross Problem</h4><p>A test cross is a way to explore the relationship between genes and their respective alleles. It is a useful tool for genetic mapping and deciphering the inheritance of traits. Specifically, a three-point test cross examines three (3) genes at the same time to learn about their assortment in gamete formation.</p><p>A standard three-point test cross involves crossing a heterozygous organism for all three genes with an organism that is homozygous recessive for all three genes</p> <p>For this problem, a test cross using a fruit fly (<i>Drosophila melanogaster</i>) heterozygous for three genes was conducted to understand their genetic interactions.</p><h6>Characteristics of Recessive Phenotypes</h6><p><ul><li><strong><span style="color: #8a4c0a;">Gene B</span></strong> is affiliated with the '<span style="color: #8a4c0a;"><i>bumpy</i></span>' phenotype. A fruit fly that is homozygous recessive for <span style="color: #8a4c0a;">Gene B</span> has a skin texture that is not smooth, but rough with small bumps all over.</li> <li><strong><span style="color: #2a6e36;">Gene R</span></strong> is related to the '<span style="color: #2a6e36;"><i>rusty</i></span>' phenotype. A fruit fly that is homozygous recessive for <span style="color: #2a6e36;">Gene R</span> has a reddish-brown color, much like rusted iron metal.</li> <li><strong><span style="color: #464cc7;">Gene T</span></strong> is linked with the '<span style="color: #464cc7;"><i>tipsy</i></span>' phenotype. A fruit fly that is homozygous recessive for <span style="color: #464cc7;">Gene T</span> moves in an erratic path, suggesting a lack of coordination, as if intoxicated.</li> </ul></p><table style="border-collapse: collapse; A fruit fly that is homozygous recessive for <span style="color: #785814;">Gene C</span> shows behavior where it always maintains a close distance to other flies.</li> <li><strong><span style="color: #00668f;">Gene R</span></strong> is associated with the '<span style="color: #00668f;"><i>rusty</i></span>' phenotype. A fruit fly that is homozygous recessive for <span style="color: #00668f;">Gene R</span> has a reddish-brown color, much like rusted iron metal.</li> <li><strong><span style="color: #863990;">Gene Y</span></strong> is associated with the '<span style="color: #863990;"><i>yucky</i></span>' phenotype. MC	<p>337d_10af</p> <h4>Three-Point Test Cross Problem</h4><p>A test cross is a way to explore the relationship between genes and their respective alleles. It is a useful tool for genetic mapping and deciphering the inheritance of traits. Specifically, a three-point test cross examines three (3) genes at the same time to learn about their assortment in gamete formation.</p><p>A standard three-point test cross involves crossing a heterozygous organism for all three genes with an organism that is homozygous recessive for all three genes</p> <p>For this problem, a test cross using a fruit fly (<i>Drosophila melanogaster</i>) heterozygous for three genes was conducted to understand their genetic interactions.</p><h6>Characteristics of Recessive Phenotypes</h6><p><ul><li><strong><span style="color: #785814;">Gene H</span></strong> is analogous to the '<span style="color: #785814;"><i>horsey</i></span>' phenotype. A fruit fly that is homozygous recessive for <span style="color: #785814;">Gene H</span> is quite big and strong-looking, much larger than your typical fruit fly.</li> <li><strong><span style="color: #1e6d55;">Gene K</span></strong> is connected with the '<span style="color: #1e6d55;"><i>kidney</i></span>' phenotype. A fruit fly that is homozygous recessive for <span style="color: #1e6d55;">Gene K</span> has a body shape that is curved, similar to a kidney bean.</li> <li><strong><span style="color: #93376b;">Gene P</span></strong> is analogous to the '<span style="color: #93376b;"><i>prickly</i></span>' phenotype. A fruit fly that is homozygous recessive for <span style="color: #93376b;">Gene P</span> is covered with sharp bristles, giving it a spiky texture.</li> </ul></p><table style="border-collapse: collapse; It is a useful tool for genetic mapping and deciphering the inheritance of traits. Specifically, a three-point test cross examines three (3) genes at the same time to learn about their assortment in gamete formation.</p><p>A standard three-point test cross involves crossing a heterozygous organism for all three genes with an organism that is homozygous recessive for all three genes</p> <p>For this problem, a test cross using a fruit fly (<i>Drosophila melanogaster</i>) heterozygous for three genes was conducted to understand their genetic interactions.</p><h6>Characteristics of Recessive Phenotypes</h6><p><ul><li><strong><span style="color: #9b3b19;">Gene E</span></strong> is linked with the '<span style="color: #9b3b19;"><i>eery</i></span>' phenotype. A fruit fly that is homozygous recessive for <span style="color: #9b3b19;">Gene E</span> appears to have something off, crooked limbs and other twisted appendages.</li> <li><strong><span style="color: #00697b;">Gene J</span></strong> is affiliated with the '<span style="color: #00697b;"><i>jerky</i></span>' phenotype. A fruit fly that is homozygous recessive for <span style="color: #00697b;">Gene J</span> moves in rapid and sudden movements, displaying an unpredictable flight pattern.</li> <li><strong><span style="color: #6d44a8;">Gene M</span></strong> is linked with the '<span style="color: #6d44a8;"><i>mushy</i></span>' phenotype. A fruit fly that is homozygous recessive for <span style="color: #6d44a8;">Gene M</span> feels soft to the touch and unusually squishy, unlike the usual firmness.</li> </ul></p><table style="border-collapse: collapse; MC	<p>ec98_23c4</p> <h4>Three-Point Test Cross Problem</h4><p>A test cross is a way to explore the relationship between genes and their respective alleles. It is a useful tool for genetic mapping and deciphering the inheritance of traits. Specifically, a three-point test cross examines three (3) genes at the same time to learn about their assortment in gamete formation.</p><p>A standard three-point test cross involves crossing a heterozygous organism for all three genes with an organism that is homozygous recessive for all three genes</p> <p>For this problem, a test cross using a fruit fly (<i>Drosophila melanogaster</i>) heterozygous for three genes was conducted to understand their genetic interactions.</p><h6>Characteristics of Recessive Phenotypes</h6><p><ul><li><strong><span style="color: #9b3b19;">Gene A</span></strong> is associated with the '<span style="color: #9b3b19;"><i>artsy</i></span>' phenotype. A fruit fly that is homozygous recessive for <span style="color: #9b3b19;">Gene A</span> has wings that are colorful and distinctive patterns.</li> <li><strong><span style="color: #1e6d55;">Gene C</span></strong> is related to the '<span style="color: #1e6d55;"><i>chummy</i></span>' phenotype. A fruit fly that is homozygous recessive for <span style="color: #1e6d55;">Gene C</span> shows behavior where it always maintains a close distance to other flies.</li> <li><strong><span style="color: #464cc7;">Gene D</span></strong> is associated with the '<span style="color: #464cc7;"><i>dewy</i></span>' phenotype. A fruit fly that is homozygous recessive for <span style="color: #464cc7;">Gene D</span> appears moist, with its body covered in tiny droplets of water.</li> </ul></p><table style="border-collapse: collapse; Specifically, a three-point test cross examines three (3) genes at the same time to learn about their assortment in gamete formation.</p><p>A standard three-point test cross involves crossing a heterozygous organism for all three genes with an organism that is homozygous recessive for all three genes</p> <p>For this problem, a test cross using a fruit fly (<i>Drosophila melanogaster</i>) heterozygous for three genes was conducted to understand their genetic interactions.</p><h6>Characteristics of Recessive Phenotypes</h6><p><ul><li><strong><span style="color: #51671f;">Gene D</span></strong> is analogous to the '<span style="color: #51671f;"><i>dewy</i></span>' phenotype. MC	<p>cb4b_9bcc</p> <h4>Three-Point Test Cross Problem</h4><p>A test cross is a way to explore the relationship between genes and their respective alleles. It is a useful tool for genetic mapping and deciphering the inheritance of traits. Specifically, a three-point test cross examines three (3) genes at the same time to learn about their assortment in gamete formation.</p><p>A standard three-point test cross involves crossing a heterozygous organism for all three genes with an organism that is homozygous recessive for all three genes</p> <p>For this problem, a test cross using a fruit fly (<i>Drosophila melanogaster</i>) heterozygous for three genes was conducted to understand their genetic interactions.</p><h6>Characteristics of Recessive Phenotypes</h6><p><ul><li><strong><span style="color: #9b3b19;">Gene F</span></strong> is linked with the '<span style="color: #9b3b19;"><i>fuzzy</i></span>' phenotype. A fruit fly that is homozygous recessive for <span style="color: #9b3b19;">Gene F</span> is covered in a dense layer of hairs, giving it a soft appearance.</li> <li><strong><span style="color: #51671f;">Gene R</span></strong> is related to the '<span style="color: #51671f;"><i>rusty</i></span>' phenotype. A fruit fly that is homozygous recessive for <span style="color: #51671f;">Gene R</span> has a reddish-brown color, much like rusted iron metal.</li> <li><strong><span style="color: #6d44a8;">Gene Y</span></strong> is connected with the '<span style="color: #6d44a8;"><i>yucky</i></span>' phenotype. A fruit fly that is homozygous recessive for <span style="color: #6d44a8;">Gene Y</span> gives off an unpleasant odor and has a generally unappealing look.</li> </ul></p><table style="border-collapse: collapse; A fruit fly that is homozygous recessive for <span style="color: #8a4c0a;">Gene B</span> has a skin texture that is not smooth, but rough with small bumps all over.</li> <li><strong><span style="color: #2a6e36;">Gene P</span></strong> is correlated with the '<span style="color: #2a6e36;"><i>prickly</i></span>' phenotype. A fruit fly that is homozygous recessive for <span style="color: #2a6e36;">Gene P</span> is covered with sharp bristles, giving it a spiky texture.</li> <li><strong><span style="color: #863990;">Gene X</span></strong> is related to the '<span style="color: #863990;"><i>xanthic</i></span>' phenotype. MC	<p>b5f4_c58a</p> <h4>Three-Point Test Cross Problem</h4><p>A test cross is a way to explore the relationship between genes and their respective alleles. It is a useful tool for genetic mapping and deciphering the inheritance of traits. Specifically, a three-point test cross examines three (3) genes at the same time to learn about their assortment in gamete formation.</p><p>A standard three-point test cross involves crossing a heterozygous organism for all three genes with an organism that is homozygous recessive for all three genes</p> <p>For this problem, a test cross using a fruit fly (<i>Drosophila melanogaster</i>) heterozygous for three genes was conducted to understand their genetic interactions.</p><h6>Characteristics of Recessive Phenotypes</h6><p><ul><li><strong><span style="color: #695f00;">Gene K</span></strong> is linked with the '<span style="color: #695f00;"><i>kidney</i></span>' phenotype. A fruit fly that is homozygous recessive for <span style="color: #695f00;">Gene K</span> has a body shape that is curved, similar to a kidney bean.</li> <li><strong><span style="color: #006c69;">Gene T</span></strong> is connected with the '<span style="color: #006c69;"><i>tipsy</i></span>' phenotype. A fruit fly that is homozygous recessive for <span style="color: #006c69;">Gene T</span> moves in an erratic path, suggesting a lack of coordination, as if intoxicated.</li> <li><strong><span style="color: #93376b;">Gene X</span></strong> is connected with the '<span style="color: #93376b;"><i>xanthic</i></span>' phenotype. A fruit fly that is homozygous recessive for <span style="color: #93376b;">Gene X</span> has a fluorescent bright yellow coloring.</li> </ul></p><table style="border-collapse: collapse; border: 2px solid black;"><tr><th align="center" style="border: 1px solid black; MC	<p>a612_baf0</p> <h4>Three-Point Test Cross Problem</h4><p>A test cross is a way to explore the relationship between genes and their respective alleles. It is a useful tool for genetic mapping and deciphering the inheritance of traits. Specifically, a three-point test cross examines three (3) genes at the same time to learn about their assortment in gamete formation.</p><p>A standard three-point test cross involves crossing a heterozygous organism for all three genes with an organism that is homozygous recessive for all three genes</p> <p>For this problem, a test cross using a fruit fly (<i>Drosophila melanogaster</i>) heterozygous for three genes was conducted to understand their genetic interactions.</p><h6>Characteristics of Recessive Phenotypes</h6><p><ul><li><strong><span style="color: #9e3243;">Gene A</span></strong> is analogous to the '<span style="color: #9e3243;"><i>artsy</i></span>' phenotype. A fruit fly that is homozygous recessive for <span style="color: #9e3243;">Gene A</span> has wings that are colorful and distinctive patterns.</li> <li><strong><span style="color: #1e6d55;">Gene E</span></strong> is linked with the '<span style="color: #1e6d55;"><i>eery</i></span>' phenotype. A fruit fly that is homozygous recessive for <span style="color: #1e6d55;">Gene E</span> appears to have something off, crooked limbs and other twisted appendages.</li> <li><strong><span style="color: #2d5f9f;">Gene W</span></strong> is correlated with the '<span style="color: #2d5f9f;"><i>waxy</i></span>' phenotype. A fruit fly that is homozygous recessive for <span style="color: #2d5f9f;">Gene W</span> has a thick protective layer that is water resistant and opague.</li> </ul></p><table style="border-collapse: collapse; MC	<p>a312_23c4</p> <h4>Three-Point Test Cross Problem</h4><p>A test cross is a way to explore the relationship between genes and their respective alleles. It is a useful tool for genetic mapping and deciphering the inheritance of traits. Specifically, a three-point test cross examines three (3) genes at the same time to learn about their assortment in gamete formation.</p><p>A standard three-point test cross involves crossing a heterozygous organism for all three genes with an organism that is homozygous recessive for all three genes</p> <p>For this problem, a test cross using a fruit fly (<i>Drosophila melanogaster</i>) heterozygous for three genes was conducted to understand their genetic interactions.</p><h6>Characteristics of Recessive Phenotypes</h6><p><ul><li><strong><span style="color: #9b3b19;">Gene A</span></strong> is analogous to the '<span style="color: #9b3b19;"><i>artsy</i></span>' phenotype. A fruit fly that is homozygous recessive for <span style="color: #9b3b19;">Gene A</span> has wings that are colorful and distinctive patterns.</li> <li><strong><span style="color: #51671f;">Gene D</span></strong> is analogous to the '<span style="color: #51671f;"><i>dewy</i></span>' phenotype. A fruit fly that is homozygous recessive for <span style="color: #51671f;">Gene D</span> appears moist, with its body covered in tiny droplets of water.</li> <li><strong><span style="color: #2d5f9f;">Gene F</span></strong> is related to the '<span style="color: #2d5f9f;"><i>fuzzy</i></span>' phenotype. A fruit fly that is homozygous recessive for <span style="color: #2d5f9f;">Gene F</span> is covered in a dense layer of hairs, giving it a soft appearance.</li> </ul></p><table style="border-collapse: collapse; Specifically, a three-point test cross examines three (3) genes at the same time to learn about their assortment in gamete formation.</p><p>A standard three-point test cross involves crossing a heterozygous organism for all three genes with an organism that is homozygous recessive for all three genes</p> <p>For this problem, a test cross using a fruit fly (<i>Drosophila melanogaster</i>) heterozygous for three genes was conducted to understand their genetic interactions.</p><h6>Characteristics of Recessive Phenotypes</h6><p><ul><li><strong><span style="color: #2a6e36;">Gene B</span></strong> is linked with the '<span style="color: #2a6e36;"><i>bumpy</i></span>' phenotype. A fruit fly that is homozygous recessive for <span style="color: #2a6e36;">Gene B</span> has a skin texture that is not smooth, but rough with small bumps all over.</li> <li><strong><span style="color: #00668f;">Gene R</span></strong> is analogous to the '<span style="color: #00668f;"><i>rusty</i></span>' phenotype. A fruit fly that is homozygous recessive for <span style="color: #00668f;">Gene R</span> has a reddish-brown color, much like rusted iron metal.</li> <li><strong><span style="color: #863990;">Gene W</span></strong> is correlated with the '<span style="color: #863990;"><i>waxy</i></span>' phenotype. A fruit fly that is homozygous recessive for <span style="color: #863990;">Gene W</span> has a thick protective layer that is water resistant and opague.</li> </ul></p><table style="border-collapse: collapse; It is a useful tool for genetic mapping and deciphering the inheritance of traits. Specifically, a three-point test cross examines three (3) genes at the same time to learn about their assortment in gamete formation.</p><p>A standard three-point test cross involves crossing a heterozygous organism for all three genes with an organism that is homozygous recessive for all three genes</p> <p>For this problem, a test cross using a fruit fly (<i>Drosophila melanogaster</i>) heterozygous for three genes was conducted to understand their genetic interactions.</p><h6>Characteristics of Recessive Phenotypes</h6><p><ul><li><strong><span style="color: #8a4c0a;">Gene B</span></strong> is correlated with the '<span style="color: #8a4c0a;"><i>bumpy</i></span>' phenotype. A fruit fly that is homozygous recessive for <span style="color: #8a4c0a;">Gene B</span> has a skin texture that is not smooth, but rough with small bumps all over.</li> <li><strong><span style="color: #2a6e36;">Gene C</span></strong> is linked with the '<span style="color: #2a6e36;"><i>chummy</i></span>' phenotype. A fruit fly that is homozygous recessive for <span style="color: #2a6e36;">Gene C</span> shows behavior where it always maintains a close distance to other flies.</li> <li><strong><span style="color: #464cc7;">Gene T</span></strong> is related to the '<span style="color: #464cc7;"><i>tipsy</i></span>' phenotype. MC	<p>e49d_10af</p> <h4>Three-Point Test Cross Problem</h4><p>A test cross is a way to explore the relationship between genes and their respective alleles. It is a useful tool for genetic mapping and deciphering the inheritance of traits. Specifically, a three-point test cross examines three (3) genes at the same time to learn about their assortment in gamete formation.</p><p>A standard three-point test cross involves crossing a heterozygous organism for all three genes with an organism that is homozygous recessive for all three genes</p> <p>For this problem, a test cross using a fruit fly (<i>Drosophila melanogaster</i>) heterozygous for three genes was conducted to understand their genetic interactions.</p><h6>Characteristics of Recessive Phenotypes</h6><p><ul><li><strong><span style="color: #785814;">Gene H</span></strong> is correlated with the '<span style="color: #785814;"><i>horsey</i></span>' phenotype. A fruit fly that is homozygous recessive for <span style="color: #785814;">Gene H</span> is quite big and strong-looking, much larger than your typical fruit fly.</li> <li><strong><span style="color: #00668f;">Gene M</span></strong> is correlated with the '<span style="color: #00668f;"><i>mushy</i></span>' phenotype. A fruit fly that is homozygous recessive for <span style="color: #00668f;">Gene M</span> feels soft to the touch and unusually squishy, unlike the usual firmness.</li> <li><strong><span style="color: #93376b;">Gene P</span></strong> is correlated with the '<span style="color: #93376b;"><i>prickly</i></span>' phenotype. A fruit fly that is homozygous recessive for <span style="color: #93376b;">Gene P</span> is covered with sharp bristles, giving it a spiky texture.</li> </ul></p><table style="border-collapse: collapse; MC	<p>ae02_23c4</p> <h4>Three-Point Test Cross Problem</h4><p>A test cross is a way to explore the relationship between genes and their respective alleles. It is a useful tool for genetic mapping and deciphering the inheritance of traits. Specifically, a three-point test cross examines three (3) genes at the same time to learn about their assortment in gamete formation.</p><p>A standard three-point test cross involves crossing a heterozygous organism for all three genes with an organism that is homozygous recessive for all three genes</p> <p>For this problem, a test cross using a fruit fly (<i>Drosophila melanogaster</i>) heterozygous for three genes was conducted to understand their genetic interactions.</p><h6>Characteristics of Recessive Phenotypes</h6><p><ul><li><strong><span style="color: #9b3b19;">Gene H</span></strong> is correlated with the '<span style="color: #9b3b19;"><i>horsey</i></span>' phenotype. A fruit fly that is homozygous recessive for <span style="color: #9b3b19;">Gene H</span> is quite big and strong-looking, much larger than your typical fruit fly.</li> <li><strong><span style="color: #2a6e36;">Gene P</span></strong> is connected with the '<span style="color: #2a6e36;"><i>prickly</i></span>' phenotype. A fruit fly that is homozygous recessive for <span style="color: #2a6e36;">Gene P</span> is covered with sharp bristles, giving it a spiky texture.</li> <li><strong><span style="color: #464cc7;">Gene W</span></strong> is correlated with the '<span style="color: #464cc7;"><i>waxy</i></span>' phenotype. A fruit fly that is homozygous recessive for <span style="color: #464cc7;">Gene W</span> has a thick protective layer that is water resistant and opague.</li> </ul></p><table style="border-collapse: collapse; It is a useful tool for genetic mapping and deciphering the inheritance of traits. Specifically, a three-point test cross examines three (3) genes at the same time to learn about their assortment in gamete formation.</p><p>A standard three-point test cross involves crossing a heterozygous organism for all three genes with an organism that is homozygous recessive for all three genes</p> <p>For this problem, a test cross using a fruit fly (<i>Drosophila melanogaster</i>) heterozygous for three genes was conducted to understand their genetic interactions.</p><h6>Characteristics of Recessive Phenotypes</h6><p><ul><li><strong><span style="color: #8a4c0a;">Gene A</span></strong> is connected with the '<span style="color: #8a4c0a;"><i>artsy</i></span>' phenotype. A fruit fly that is homozygous recessive for <span style="color: #8a4c0a;">Gene A</span> has wings that are colorful and distinctive patterns.</li> <li><strong><span style="color: #006c69;">Gene P</span></strong> is analogous to the '<span style="color: #006c69;"><i>prickly</i></span>' phenotype. A fruit fly that is homozygous recessive for <span style="color: #006c69;">Gene P</span> is covered with sharp bristles, giving it a spiky texture.</li> <li><strong><span style="color: #6d44a8;">Gene X</span></strong> is correlated with the '<span style="color: #6d44a8;"><i>xanthic</i></span>' phenotype. A fruit fly that is homozygous recessive for <span style="color: #6d44a8;">Gene X</span> has a fluorescent bright yellow coloring.</li> </ul></p><table style="border-collapse: collapse; border: 2px solid black;"><tr><th align="center" style="border: 1px solid black; It is a useful tool for genetic mapping and deciphering the inheritance of traits. Specifically, a three-point test cross examines three (3) genes at the same time to learn about their assortment in gamete formation.</p><p>A standard three-point test cross involves crossing a heterozygous organism for all three genes with an organism that is homozygous recessive for all three genes</p> <p>For this problem, a test cross using a fruit fly (<i>Drosophila melanogaster</i>) heterozygous for three genes was conducted to understand their genetic interactions.</p><h6>Characteristics of Recessive Phenotypes</h6><p><ul><li><strong><span style="color: #8a4c0a;">Gene D</span></strong> is associated with the '<span style="color: #8a4c0a;"><i>dewy</i></span>' phenotype. A fruit fly that is homozygous recessive for <span style="color: #8a4c0a;">Gene D</span> appears moist, with its body covered in tiny droplets of water.</li> <li><strong><span style="color: #1e6d55;">Gene M</span></strong> is related to the '<span style="color: #1e6d55;"><i>mushy</i></span>' phenotype. A fruit fly that is homozygous recessive for <span style="color: #1e6d55;">Gene M</span> feels soft to the touch and unusually squishy, unlike the usual firmness.</li> <li><strong><span style="color: #464cc7;">Gene R</span></strong> is affiliated with the '<span style="color: #464cc7;"><i>rusty</i></span>' phenotype. A fruit fly that is homozygous recessive for <span style="color: #464cc7;">Gene R</span> has a reddish-brown color, much like rusted iron metal.</li> </ul></p><table style="border-collapse: collapse; It is a useful tool for genetic mapping and deciphering the inheritance of traits. Specifically, a three-point test cross examines three (3) genes at the same time to learn about their assortment in gamete formation.</p><p>A standard three-point test cross involves crossing a heterozygous organism for all three genes with an organism that is homozygous recessive for all three genes</p> <p>For this problem, a test cross using a fruit fly (<i>Drosophila melanogaster</i>) heterozygous for three genes was conducted to understand their genetic interactions.</p><h6>Characteristics of Recessive Phenotypes</h6><p><ul><li><strong><span style="color: #785814;">Gene B</span></strong> is correlated with the '<span style="color: #785814;"><i>bumpy</i></span>' phenotype. A fruit fly that is homozygous recessive for <span style="color: #785814;">Gene B</span> has a skin texture that is not smooth, but rough with small bumps all over.</li> <li><strong><span style="color: #00668f;">Gene C</span></strong> is associated with the '<span style="color: #00668f;"><i>chummy</i></span>' phenotype. A fruit fly that is homozygous recessive for <span style="color: #00668f;">Gene C</span> shows behavior where it always maintains a close distance to other flies.</li> <li><strong><span style="color: #863990;">Gene E</span></strong> is related to the '<span style="color: #863990;"><i>eery</i></span>' phenotype. A fruit fly that is homozygous recessive for <span style="color: #863990;">Gene E</span> appears to have something off, crooked limbs and other twisted appendages.</li> </ul></p><table style="border-collapse: collapse; MC	<p>8bf3_c58a</p> <h4>Three-Point Test Cross Problem</h4><p>A test cross is a way to explore the relationship between genes and their respective alleles. It is a useful tool for genetic mapping and deciphering the inheritance of traits. Specifically, a three-point test cross examines three (3) genes at the same time to learn about their assortment in gamete formation.</p><p>A standard three-point test cross involves crossing a heterozygous organism for all three genes with an organism that is homozygous recessive for all three genes</p> <p>For this problem, a test cross using a fruit fly (<i>Drosophila melanogaster</i>) heterozygous for three genes was conducted to understand their genetic interactions.</p><h6>Characteristics of Recessive Phenotypes</h6><p><ul><li><strong><span style="color: #695f00;">Gene A</span></strong> is related to the '<span style="color: #695f00;"><i>artsy</i></span>' phenotype. A fruit fly that is homozygous recessive for <span style="color: #695f00;">Gene A</span> has wings that are colorful and distinctive patterns.</li> <li><strong><span style="color: #00697b;">Gene C</span></strong> is associated with the '<span style="color: #00697b;"><i>chummy</i></span>' phenotype. A fruit fly that is homozygous recessive for <span style="color: #00697b;">Gene C</span> shows behavior where it always maintains a close distance to other flies.</li> <li><strong><span style="color: #93376b;">Gene K</span></strong> is connected with the '<span style="color: #93376b;"><i>kidney</i></span>' phenotype. A fruit fly that is homozygous recessive for <span style="color: #93376b;">Gene K</span> has a body shape that is curved, similar to a kidney bean.</li> </ul></p><table style="border-collapse: collapse; MC	<p>d28a_c58a</p> <h4>Three-Point Test Cross Problem</h4><p>A test cross is a way to explore the relationship between genes and their respective alleles. It is a useful tool for genetic mapping and deciphering the inheritance of traits. Specifically, a three-point test cross examines three (3) genes at the same time to learn about their assortment in gamete formation.</p><p>A standard three-point test cross involves crossing a heterozygous organism for all three genes with an organism that is homozygous recessive for all three genes</p> <p>For this problem, a test cross using a fruit fly (<i>Drosophila melanogaster</i>) heterozygous for three genes was conducted to understand their genetic interactions.</p><h6>Characteristics of Recessive Phenotypes</h6><p><ul><li><strong><span style="color: #8a4c0a;">Gene B</span></strong> is associated with the '<span style="color: #8a4c0a;"><i>bumpy</i></span>' phenotype. A fruit fly that is homozygous recessive for <span style="color: #8a4c0a;">Gene B</span> has a skin texture that is not smooth, but rough with small bumps all over.</li> <li><strong><span style="color: #006c69;">Gene E</span></strong> is affiliated with the '<span style="color: #006c69;"><i>eery</i></span>' phenotype. A fruit fly that is homozygous recessive for <span style="color: #006c69;">Gene E</span> appears to have something off, crooked limbs and other twisted appendages.</li> <li><strong><span style="color: #464cc7;">Gene F</span></strong> is related to the '<span style="color: #464cc7;"><i>fuzzy</i></span>' phenotype. A fruit fly that is homozygous recessive for <span style="color: #464cc7;">Gene F</span> is covered in a dense layer of hairs, giving it a soft appearance.</li> </ul></p><table style="border-collapse: collapse; Specifically, a three-point test cross examines three (3) genes at the same time to learn about their assortment in gamete formation.</p><p>A standard three-point test cross involves crossing a heterozygous organism for all three genes with an organism that is homozygous recessive for all three genes</p> <p>For this problem, a test cross using a fruit fly (<i>Drosophila melanogaster</i>) heterozygous for three genes was conducted to understand their genetic interactions.</p><h6>Characteristics of Recessive Phenotypes</h6><p><ul><li><strong><span style="color: #9e3243;">Gene A</span></strong> is correlated with the '<span style="color: #9e3243;"><i>artsy</i></span>' phenotype. A fruit fly that is homozygous recessive for <span style="color: #9e3243;">Gene A</span> has wings that are colorful and distinctive patterns.</li> <li><strong><span style="color: #695f00;">Gene E</span></strong> is correlated with the '<span style="color: #695f00;"><i>eery</i></span>' phenotype. A fruit fly that is homozygous recessive for <span style="color: #695f00;">Gene E</span> appears to have something off, crooked limbs and other twisted appendages.</li> <li><strong><span style="color: #00668f;">Gene K</span></strong> is related to the '<span style="color: #00668f;"><i>kidney</i></span>' phenotype. A fruit fly that is homozygous recessive for <span style="color: #00668f;">Gene K</span> has a body shape that is curved, similar to a kidney bean.</li> </ul></p><table style="border-collapse: collapse; It is a useful tool for genetic mapping and deciphering the inheritance of traits. Specifically, a three-point test cross examines three (3) genes at the same time to learn about their assortment in gamete formation.</p><p>A standard three-point test cross involves crossing a heterozygous organism for all three genes with an organism that is homozygous recessive for all three genes</p> <p>For this problem, a test cross using a fruit fly (<i>Drosophila melanogaster</i>) heterozygous for three genes was conducted to understand their genetic interactions.</p><h6>Characteristics of Recessive Phenotypes</h6><p><ul><li><strong><span style="color: #695f00;">Gene M</span></strong> is related to the '<span style="color: #695f00;"><i>mushy</i></span>' phenotype. A fruit fly that is homozygous recessive for <span style="color: #695f00;">Gene M</span> feels soft to the touch and unusually squishy, unlike the usual firmness.</li> <li><strong><span style="color: #00668f;">Gene N</span></strong> is analogous to the '<span style="color: #00668f;"><i>nerdy</i></span>' phenotype. A fruit fly that is homozygous recessive for <span style="color: #00668f;">Gene N</span> has large, prominent eyes that stand out, much like thick-rimmed glasses.</li> <li><strong><span style="color: #863990;">Gene Y</span></strong> is analogous to the '<span style="color: #863990;"><i>yucky</i></span>' phenotype. A fruit fly that is homozygous recessive for <span style="color: #863990;">Gene Y</span> gives off an unpleasant odor and has a generally unappealing look.</li> </ul></p><table style="border-collapse: collapse; MC	<p>9420_aae3</p> <h4>Three-Point Test Cross Problem</h4><p>A test cross is a way to explore the relationship between genes and their respective alleles. It is a useful tool for genetic mapping and deciphering the inheritance of traits. Specifically, a three-point test cross examines three (3) genes at the same time to learn about their assortment in gamete formation.</p><p>A standard three-point test cross involves crossing a heterozygous organism for all three genes with an organism that is homozygous recessive for all three genes</p> <p>For this problem, a test cross using a fruit fly (<i>Drosophila melanogaster</i>) heterozygous for three genes was conducted to understand their genetic interactions.</p><h6>Characteristics of Recessive Phenotypes</h6><p><ul><li><strong><span style="color: #8a4c0a;">Gene A</span></strong> is connected with the '<span style="color: #8a4c0a;"><i>artsy</i></span>' phenotype. A fruit fly that is homozygous recessive for <span style="color: #8a4c0a;">Gene A</span> has wings that are colorful and distinctive patterns.</li> <li><strong><span style="color: #2a6e36;">Gene C</span></strong> is correlated with the '<span style="color: #2a6e36;"><i>chummy</i></span>' phenotype. A fruit fly that is homozygous recessive for <span style="color: #2a6e36;">Gene C</span> shows behavior where it always maintains a close distance to other flies.</li> <li><strong><span style="color: #863990;">Gene R</span></strong> is affiliated with the '<span style="color: #863990;"><i>rusty</i></span>' phenotype. A fruit fly that is homozygous recessive for <span style="color: #863990;">Gene R</span> has a reddish-brown color, much like rusted iron metal.</li> </ul></p><table style="border-collapse: collapse; It is a useful tool for genetic mapping and deciphering the inheritance of traits. Specifically, a three-point test cross examines three (3) genes at the same time to learn about their assortment in gamete formation.</p><p>A standard three-point test cross involves crossing a heterozygous organism for all three genes with an organism that is homozygous recessive for all three genes</p> <p>For this problem, a test cross using a fruit fly (<i>Drosophila melanogaster</i>) heterozygous for three genes was conducted to understand their genetic interactions.</p><h6>Characteristics of Recessive Phenotypes</h6><p><ul><li><strong><span style="color: #9e3243;">Gene D</span></strong> is affiliated with the '<span style="color: #9e3243;"><i>dewy</i></span>' phenotype. A fruit fly that is homozygous recessive for <span style="color: #9e3243;">Gene D</span> appears moist, with its body covered in tiny droplets of water.</li> <li><strong><span style="color: #695f00;">Gene J</span></strong> is correlated with the '<span style="color: #695f00;"><i>jerky</i></span>' phenotype. A fruit fly that is homozygous recessive for <span style="color: #695f00;">Gene J</span> moves in rapid and sudden movements, displaying an unpredictable flight pattern.</li> <li><strong><span style="color: #00697b;">Gene R</span></strong> is analogous to the '<span style="color: #00697b;"><i>rusty</i></span>' phenotype. A fruit fly that is homozygous recessive for <span style="color: #00697b;">Gene R</span> has a reddish-brown color, much like rusted iron metal.</li> </ul></p><table style="border-collapse: collapse; MC	<p>a0b6_38cc</p> <h4>Three-Point Test Cross Problem</h4><p>A test cross is a way to explore the relationship between genes and their respective alleles. It is a useful tool for genetic mapping and deciphering the inheritance of traits. Specifically, a three-point test cross examines three (3) genes at the same time to learn about their assortment in gamete formation.</p><p>A standard three-point test cross involves crossing a heterozygous organism for all three genes with an organism that is homozygous recessive for all three genes</p> <p>For this problem, a test cross using a fruit fly (<i>Drosophila melanogaster</i>) heterozygous for three genes was conducted to understand their genetic interactions.</p><h6>Characteristics of Recessive Phenotypes</h6><p><ul><li><strong><span style="color: #9e3243;">Gene A</span></strong> is affiliated with the '<span style="color: #9e3243;"><i>artsy</i></span>' phenotype. A fruit fly that is homozygous recessive for <span style="color: #9e3243;">Gene A</span> has wings that are colorful and distinctive patterns.</li> <li><strong><span style="color: #51671f;">Gene J</span></strong> is connected with the '<span style="color: #51671f;"><i>jerky</i></span>' phenotype. A fruit fly that is homozygous recessive for <span style="color: #51671f;">Gene J</span> moves in rapid and sudden movements, displaying an unpredictable flight pattern.</li> <li><strong><span style="color: #00697b;">Gene K</span></strong> is related to the '<span style="color: #00697b;"><i>kidney</i></span>' phenotype. A fruit fly that is homozygous recessive for <span style="color: #00697b;">Gene K</span> has a body shape that is curved, similar to a kidney bean.</li> </ul></p><table style="border-collapse: collapse; Specifically, a three-point test cross examines three (3) genes at the same time to learn about their assortment in gamete formation.</p><p>A standard three-point test cross involves crossing a heterozygous organism for all three genes with an organism that is homozygous recessive for all three genes</p> <p>For this problem, a test cross using a fruit fly (<i>Drosophila melanogaster</i>) heterozygous for three genes was conducted to understand their genetic interactions.</p><h6>Characteristics of Recessive Phenotypes</h6><p><ul><li><strong><span style="color: #695f00;">Gene A</span></strong> is connected with the '<span style="color: #695f00;"><i>artsy</i></span>' phenotype. A fruit fly that is homozygous recessive for <span style="color: #695f00;">Gene A</span> has wings that are colorful and distinctive patterns.</li> <li><strong><span style="color: #00697b;">Gene E</span></strong> is linked with the '<span style="color: #00697b;"><i>eery</i></span>' phenotype. A fruit fly that is homozygous recessive for <span style="color: #00697b;">Gene E</span> appears to have something off, crooked limbs and other twisted appendages.</li> <li><strong><span style="color: #93376b;">Gene M</span></strong> is correlated with the '<span style="color: #93376b;"><i>mushy</i></span>' phenotype. A fruit fly that is homozygous recessive for <span style="color: #93376b;">Gene M</span> feels soft to the touch and unusually squishy, unlike the usual firmness.</li> </ul></p><table style="border-collapse: collapse; color: #000000;">6,600</span></td></tr></table><p><ul> <li>The distance between genes A and E is 14 cM</li><li>The distance between genes A and M is 37 cM</li><li>The distance between genes E and M is 30 cM</li><li>The correct gene order determined from these distances is <strong>AEM</strong></li></ul></p> <h6>Step-by-Step Instructions for Calculating Interference</h6><ul> <li>Step 1: Count the observed number of double crossovers from the data table.</li> <li>Step 2: Calculate the probability of independent crossovers between distant genes.</li> <ul><li>Multiply the two individual crossover probabilities (based on their distance) for both adjacent gene pairs.</li></ul> <li>Step 3: Determine the expected number of double crossovers.</li> <ul><li>Multiply the combined probability (from Step 2) by the total progeny count.</li></ul> <li>Step 4: Calculate the Coefficient of Coincidence (CoC).</li> <ul><li>Divide the observed number of double crossovers (from Step 1) by the expected number (from Step 3).</li></ul> <li>Step 5: Calculate Interference.</li> <ul><li>Interference is given by the formula: Interference = 1 - CoC.</li></ul></ul><p>In genetic studies, interference refers to the phenomenon where the occurrence of a crossover in one region of a chromosome reduces the likelihood of another crossover occurring nearby, thereby affecting the expected genetic ratios.</p> <h6>Question</h6> <p>Based on the traits expressed in the offspring, <strong>select the correct fraction that represents the interference level</strong> between genes A and M.</p>	no interference, 0%	Incorrect	<sup>1</sup>/<sub>6</sub> = 0.1667 = 17%	Correct	<sup>1</sup>/<sub>3</sub> = 0.3333 = 33%	Incorrect	<sup>1</sup>/<sub>2</sub> = 0.5000 = 50%	Incorrect	<sup>2</sup>/<sub>3</sub> = 0.6667 = 67%	Incorrect	<sup>5</sup>/<sub>6</sub> = 0.8333 = 83%	Incorrect	complete interference, 100%	Incorrect
